I pretty much just eat what I need to get through the day, you know? I don't ever sit down and eat a whole lot all at once. I try to keep my portions pretty controlled.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you exercise to burn off calories?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 37 / painting_worker / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared.   },

No, I don't really do that. I mean, my job is pretty physical—I'm a painter, so I'm up and down ladders and moving gear around all day. That keeps me active. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because I had an insurance physical recently, and the doctor doing it told me my weight was too low. I don't really agree with that, honestly. To me, I just look lean from being up on ladders and working long days.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do. I know my friends and that doctor from the physical are worried about it, but I really feel like they're just overreacting. I'm still showing up to all my jobs, I'm getting my work done, and I feel fine. So, I don't see the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I said a minute ago, I really just think I'm lean. I definitely don't look at myself and see someone who is underweight. I'm just carrying the weight that makes sense for how much physical work I do every day. So, no, I don't think I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I am. I just like keeping things where they are. Like, if the scale goes up even a little bit—say, after a client dinner or eating something heavy with bread—I definitely get tense about it. When that happens, I usually start cutting back the next day just to make sure the extra weight doesn't stick. I don't really agree with that, honestly. To me, I just look lean from being up on ladders and working long days.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do. I know my friends and that doctor from the physical are worried about it, but I really feel like they're just overreacting. I'm still showing up to all my jobs, I'm getting my work done, and I feel fine. So, I don't see the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I said a minute ago, I really just think I'm lean. I definitely don't look at myself and see someone who is underweight. I'm just carrying the weight that makes sense for how much physical work I do every day. So, no, I don't think I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I am. I just like keeping things where they are. Like, if the scale goes up even a little bit—say, after a client dinner or eating something heavy with bread—I definitely get tense about it. When that happens, I usually start cutting back the next day just to make sure the extra weight doesn't stick. So, yeah, I do worry about gaining.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, my weight has been pretty steady for a while now. I haven't noticed any big drops recently or anything like that. It's just been staying around the same number.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much just eat what I need to get through the day, you know? I don't ever sit down and eat a whole lot all at once. I try to keep my portions pretty controlled.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, my job is pretty physical—I'm a painter, so I'm up and down ladders and moving gear around all day. That keeps me active. But I don't go out of my way to run or hit the gym just to burn off what I ate. Honestly, by the time I get off work, I'm usually too tired for any extra workouts anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends and the doctor who did your physical have a more accurate view of your weight than you do?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know my friends and the doctor who did my physical are worried about my weight. But honestly, I feel like they're just overreacting. I'm still showing up to my jobs every day, climbing ladders, and getting all my work done without any issues. So, I don't really see it the way they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as other people your height and age?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm probably a bit lighter than most guys my age and height. I mean, I see other guys on the job site and I know I'm definitely on the thinner side compared to them. But I don't really know exactly what they weigh, so it's hard to say for sure. I just know what feels right for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... when I look in the mirror, I don't see someone who's underweight. I just see myself. I know I'm lean, but to me, I just look normal. I don't look at my reflection and think there's a problem or that I look sick or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a menstrual period in the last few months?\n\nPatient: Uh, well... I'm a guy, so no, I don't get menstrual periods.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight influence what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely does. So, I'll usually start cutting back on what I eat the next day just to make sure the weight doesn't stick.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight make you avoid certain foods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. I avoid a lot of things, actually. Most days I just keep it to coffee, and maybe a protein bar between paint jobs. Then I'll just have a really small dinner. I just feel like... well, eating a full meal feels like it would slow me down too much, so I just stick to that routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the types of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, I guess I kind of just answered that. I don't really have specific types of food that I completely ban or anything. It's more just... I stick to the routine I just mentioned with the protein bars and keeping the dinner small so the weight stays off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what mainly controls how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say it controls me. I'm not really sure. I mean, like I said, I definitely keep my meals small so I don't put on pounds... but it's also just what I'm used to doing while I'm working. So I don't know if it's mainly the fear, or if it's just my habit at this point. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is the main reason you eat so little?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe it is a bigger reason than I realize. Like... if I have to go to a client dinner or something, or if I eat something heavier like bread, and I see the scale go up even just a little bit the next morning... I get pretty tense about it. Then I'll immediately start cutting back the next day just to make sure the weight doesn't stick. So, I don't really see it the way they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as other people your height and age?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm probably a bit lighter than most guys my age and height. I mean, I see other guys on the job site and I know I'm definitely on the thinner side compared to them. But I don't really know exactly what they weigh, so it's hard to say for sure. I just know what feels right for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... when I look in the mirror, I don't see someone who's underweight. I just see myself. I know I'm lean, but to me, I just look normal. I don't look at my reflection and think there's a problem or that I look sick or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a menstrual period in the last few months?\n\nPatient: Uh, well... I'm a guy, so no, I don't get menstrual periods.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight influence what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely does. If I step on the scale and it goes up even a little bit—like maybe after a dinner with a client or if I had something with bread—I get pretty tense about it. So, I'll usually start cutting back on what I eat the next day just to make sure the weight doesn't stick.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight make you avoid certain foods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. I avoid a lot of things, actually. Most days I just keep it to coffee, and maybe a protein bar between paint jobs. Then I'll just have a really small dinner. I just feel like... well, eating a full meal feels like it would slow me down too much, so I just stick to that routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the types of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, I guess I kind of just answered that. I don't really have specific types of food that I completely ban or anything. It's more just... I stick to the routine I just mentioned with the protein bars and keeping the dinner small so the weight stays off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what mainly controls how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say it controls me. I'm not really sure. I mean, like I said, I definitely keep my meals small so I don't put on pounds... but it's also just what I'm used to doing while I'm working. So I don't know if it's mainly the fear, or if it's just my habit at this point. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is the main reason you eat so little?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe it is a bigger reason than I realize. Like... if I have to go to a client dinner or something, or if I eat something heavier like bread, and I see the scale go up even just a little bit the next morning... I get pretty tense about it. Then I'll immediately start cutting back the next day just to make sure the weight doesn't stick. So, yeah, I guess that does mean the fear is driving a lot of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fear of gaining weight is the main reason you limit your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I think I kind of just answered that, right? Like I said a second ago, yeah, it probably is the main reason. I don't want the scale to go up, so I keep my food intake low. That's pretty much what drives it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I know my friends and my regular doctor are worried about it, but... I honestly feel like they're overreacting. I'm still showing up to my painting jobs every day and getting all my work done just fine. If it was actually a serious problem, I wouldn't be able to work like I do. So, yeah, I don't really see it the way they do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't really see myself as being at a low weight when I look in the mirror. It's more like... I'm just looking for specific things. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because I had an insurance physical recently, and the doctor doing it told me my weight was too low. I don't really agree with that, honestly. To me, I just look lean from being up on ladders and working long days.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I do. I know my friends and that doctor from the physical are worried about it, but I really feel like they're just overreacting. I'm still showing up to all my jobs, I'm getting my work done, and I feel fine. So, I don't see the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. Like I said a minute ago, I really just think I'm lean. I definitely don't look at myself and see someone who is underweight. I'm just carrying the weight that makes sense for how much physical work I do every day. So, no, I don't think I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I am. I just like keeping things where they are. Like, if the scale goes up even a little bit—say, after a client dinner or eating something heavy with bread—I definitely get tense about it. When that happens, I usually start cutting back the next day just to make sure the extra weight doesn't stick. So, yeah, I do worry about gaining.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, my weight has been pretty steady for a while now. I haven't noticed any big drops recently or anything like that. It's just been staying around the same number.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much just eat what I need to get through the day, you know? I don't ever sit down and eat a whole lot all at once. I try to keep my portions pretty controlled.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, my job is pretty physical—I'm a painter, so I'm up and down ladders and moving gear around all day. That keeps me active. But I don't go out of my way to run or hit the gym just to burn off what I ate. Honestly, by the time I get off work, I'm usually too tired for any extra workouts anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your friends and the doctor who did your physical have a more accurate view of your weight than you do?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know my friends and the doctor who did my physical are worried about my weight. But honestly, I feel like they're just overreacting. I'm still showing up to my jobs every day, climbing ladders, and getting all my work done without any issues. So, I don't really see it the way they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as other people your height and age?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm probably a bit lighter than most guys my age and height. I mean, I see other guys on the job site and I know I'm definitely on the thinner side compared to them. But I don't really know exactly what they weigh, so it's hard to say for sure. I just know what feels right for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... when I look in the mirror, I don't see someone who's underweight. I just see myself. I know I'm lean, but to me, I just look normal. I don't look at my reflection and think there's a problem or that I look sick or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a menstrual period in the last few months?\n\nPatient: Uh, well... I'm a guy, so no, I don't get menstrual periods.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight influence what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely does. If I step on the scale and it goes up even a little bit—like maybe after a dinner with a client or if I had something with bread—I get pretty tense about it. So, I'll usually start cutting back on what I eat the next day just to make sure the weight doesn't stick.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of gaining weight make you avoid certain foods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does. I avoid a lot of things, actually. Most days I just keep it to coffee, and maybe a protein bar between paint jobs. Then I'll just have a really small dinner. I just feel like... well, eating a full meal feels like it would slow me down too much, so I just stick to that routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the types of food you eat to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, I guess I kind of just answered that. I don't really have specific types of food that I completely ban or anything. It's more just... I stick to the routine I just mentioned with the protein bars and keeping the dinner small so the weight stays off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is what mainly controls how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say it controls me. I'm not really sure. I mean, like I said, I definitely keep my meals small so I don't put on pounds... but it's also just what I'm used to doing while I'm working. So I don't know if it's mainly the fear, or if it's just my habit at this point. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is the main reason you eat so little?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe it is a bigger reason than I realize. Like... if I have to go to a client dinner or something, or if I eat something heavier like bread, and I see the scale go up even just a little bit the next morning... I get pretty tense about it. Then I'll immediately start cutting back the next day just to make sure the weight doesn't stick. So, yeah, I guess that does mean the fear is driving a lot of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the fear of gaining weight is the main reason you limit your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I think I kind of just answered that, right? Like I said a second ago, yeah, it probably is the main reason. I don't want the scale to go up, so I keep my food intake low. That's pretty much what drives it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is?\n\nPatient: Not really. So, yeah, I don't really see it the way they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low when you see yourself in the mirror, or do you see yourself as being at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: I don't really see myself as being at a low weight when I look in the mirror. I'd probably just keep doing what I'm doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So, yeah, I don't really see it the way they do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low when you see yourself in the mirror, or do you see yourself as being at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: I don't really see myself as being at a low weight when I look in the mirror. It's more like... I'm just looking for specific things. When I catch myself in the mirror, or if I see a photo of myself standing next to one of my murals, I'm mostly just checking my stomach and my face. I kind of judge how the whole day is going to go based on whether they look sharp and defined enough. If they don't, I just feel like I need to work harder at it. So, no, I don't look in the mirror and think I'm too thin.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still try to lose weight or maintain your current weight even if you were told by a doctor that your weight is healthy?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess if a doctor told me my weight was healthy right now, I'd probably still just want to maintain it where it is. Like I said, I'm comfortable with my routine and how I look when I check the mirror. So yeah, I don't really know if a doctor saying it's healthy would change what I do. I'd probably just keep doing what I'm doing."
